about the proxy weight, not an exact share of the atom mass; the moment proof
below never uses $\alpha_p$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
We give the review-inserted derivation rather than appeal to an average over
$c$.  Fix the block and $k$, and put $F(n)=n/\varphi(n)$ and
\[
 q_0=\lcm(g,\rad k)\leq k.
\]
Work in multiplicative boxes $u\asymp U$, $v\asymp V$.  The conditions
$(uv,k)=1$ and $-uv^{-1}\equiv a\pmod g$ restrict
$(u\bmod q_0,v\bmod q_0)$ to reduced pairs with a fixed ratio modulo $g$:
for each of the $\varphi(q_0)$ choices of $v$-class, the $u$-class is
determined modulo $g$ and free at the remaining part, so exactly
$\varphi(q_0)^2/\varphi(g)$ ordered class pairs are compatible.  Shiu's
theorem for the multiplicative function $F$, on the fixed-relative-length
interval $u\asymp U$ in one reduced class modulo $q_0$, is applicable since
$q_0\leq k\leq K$ and $U>H=K^{10}$, and gives
\[
 \sum_{\substack{u\asymp U\\u\equiv r\ (q_0)}}F(u)
 \ll \frac{U}{\varphi(q_0)}
 \exp\Big\{-\sum_{p\mid k}\frac1{p-1}\Big\}
 \asymp \frac{U}{\varphi(q_0)}\frac{\varphi(k)}k.
\]
The last comparison holds because the logarithm of the quotient is
$O(\sum_p p^{-2})$.  Multiplying the $u$- and $v$-class sums by the number
of compatible class pairs and dividing by $UV$ yields, per box pair,
\[
 \sum_{\rm box}\frac1{\varphi(u)\varphi(v)}
 \ll\frac1{\varphi(g)}\left(\frac{\varphi(k)}k\right)^2.
\]
Summing the $O((\log(z/H))^2)=O(t^2)$ box pairs gives
\begin{equation}\tag{39.11}
 \sum\frac1{\varphi(u)\varphi(v)}
 \ll\frac{(\log z)^2}{\varphi(g)}\frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^2}.
\end{equation}
For fixed $(u,v)$, Brun--Titchmarsh in the progression
$\ell\equiv-k^{-1}\pmod{4uv}$, whose modulus is
$4uv\leq4x^{1/3}$, gives
\[
 \sum_{\substack{x<\ell\leq2x\\
                  \ell\equiv-k^{-1}\ (4uv)}}\frac1\ell
 \ll\frac1{\varphi(4uv)\log x},
 \qquad
 \frac1{\varphi(4uv)}\ll\frac1{\varphi(u)\varphi(v)},
\]
where the last inequality uses $(u,v)=1$.  Combining this with (39.11)
over the boxes proves
\begin{equation}\tag{39.10}
 \sum_{\substack{A\text{ in the block}\atop
       k_A=k,\ -uv^{-1}\equiv a\ (g)}}\frac1\ell
 \ll\frac{\log x}{\varphi(g)}\frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^2},
\end{equation}
with an absolute constant, uniformly in $k\leq K$, $g\mid k$, and reduced
$a$: no $k^\varepsilon$, divisor, or logarithmic loss enters.  After the
$O(t)$ blocks and division by $k$, this is the upper half of (39.7).

For the lower half drop the ratio condition.  For one fixed $k$, a modulus
$4uv$ has at most $2^{\omega(uv)}=(\log X)^{O(1)}$ ordered allocations.
Thus ordinary Bombieri--Vinogradov, with a fixed sufficiently large
logarithmic saving, evaluates the prime progressions without the factor
$K$: $k$ is fixed on this line.  The low-$\omega$ Rankin argument from the
source proof retains a fixed proportion uniformly.  At a prime $p\mid k$
the exact coprime-pair local factor differs from $(1-1/p)^2$ by
$1+O(p^{-2})$, so the unrestricted harmonic pair mass is
$\asymp(\log(z/H))^2(\varphi(k)/k)^2$ uniformly.  This gives the matching
lower bound block by block and proves (39.7).

Finally $b(k)=(\varphi(k)/k)^2$ is multiplicative and
$\sum b(k)/k\asymp\log K$, also after selecting $k\equiv1\pmod4$, by the
principal/$\chi_4$ decomposition.  At an odd prime its local factor is
\begin{equation}\tag{39.12}
 1+\sum_{e\geq1}\frac{(1-1/p)^2}{p^e}=1+\frac{p-1}{p^2},
\end{equation}
so the part with $p\mid k$, divided by the full factor, is exactly (39.9).
\end{proof}

Given a reduced fibre $c$, an atom with multiplier $k$ is active for one of
the $\varphi(k)$ unit residues and then has prime-coordinate probability
$1/\ell$.  Averaging those conditional masses produces the factor
$1/\varphi(k)$, whereas the unconditioned cylinder has probability
$1/(k\ell)$.  Formula (39.7), not the conditional mass profile used in the
earlier moving-$c$ family, is the correct profile for factorial moments in
the $c$-free CRT space.

\subsection{Collapse versus consistency}
The rough collapse bound which discards residue consistency would charge a
shared prime $p$ by $p\alpha_p^2\asymp1/p$, producing a false
$\log\log K$ loss.  The required cancellation is exact at the level of a
new compatible atom.

For a cutoff $y$, put
\begin{equation}\tag{39.13}
 q_y(k)=\prod_{\substack{p\mid k\\p\leq y}}\frac p{p-1},
 \qquad
 b_y(g)=\prod_{\substack{p^e\Vert g\\p\leq y}}\varphi(p^e)
         \prod_{\substack{p^e\Vert g\\p>y}}p^e.
\end{equation}
Condition the CRT space by $(n,P_y)=1$, where $P_y=\prod_{p\leq y}p$.
An atom then has probability $q_y(k)/(k\ell)$.  If a compatible previously
selected set has multiplier lcm $R$, adding an atom of multiplier $k$, with
$g=(k,R)$, multiplies its intersection probability by
\begin{equation}\tag{39.14}
 \frac{q_y(k)}{k\ell}\,b_y(g).
\end{equation}
Compatibility fixes one unit residue modulo $g$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:profiles}
says that the atom mass in that residue is at most $1/\varphi(g)$ of its
multiplier mass.  Thus the collapse left after summing the compatible new
atoms is
\begin{equation}\tag{39.15}
 \frac{b_y(g)}{\varphi(g)}
 =\prod_{\substack{p\mid g\\p>y}}\frac p{p-1};
\end{equation}
small conditioned primes cancel exactly, and an unconditioned shared prime
costs only $p/(p-1)$, not $p$.

\begin{lemma}[source Lemma 39.3: uniform multiplier Euler bound; proved]
\label{lem:euler-bound}
Uniformly in $y\geq2$, every integer $R$, and $K\geq3$,
\begin{equation}\tag{39.16}
 \sum_{\substack{k\leq K\\k\equiv1(4)}}
 \frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^3}q_y(k)
 \prod_{\substack{p\mid(k,R)\\p>y}}\frac p{p-1}
 \ll\log K.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
It is enough to sum over all odd $k$.  The unmodified local nonconstant mass
is $(p-1)/p^2$.  Multiplication either by $q_y$ for $p\leq y$, or by
$p/(p-1)$ when $p>y$ and $p\mid R$, changes this to $1/p$.  The quotient
\begin{equation}\tag{39.17}
 \frac{1+1/p}{1+(p-1)/p^2}=1+O(p^{-2})
\end{equation}
has a convergent product, uniformly in the chosen set of modified primes.
The remaining harmonic Euler product is $O(\log K)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[source Theorem 39.4: all required upper factorial moments;
proved, provisional]\label{thm:factorial-cfree}
In the CRT space conditioned by $(n,P_y)=1$, for every integer $m\geq1$,
\begin{equation}\tag{39.18}
 \mathbb E(H_X)_m\leq(Ct^3)^m,
\end{equation}
with $C$ depending at most on $\kappa$, uniformly in
$2\leq y<X^{1/2}$.  The upper restriction is necessary: for $y\geq\ell$
the coprimality conditioning would renormalize the $\ell$-coordinate itself
by $\ell/(\ell-1)$, which (39.14) does not include.  The application below
has $y=Bt^3<X^{1/2}$ for all sufficiently large $X$.  In particular the
bound holds through every order $m=D_Bt^3$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Expand the ordered factorial moment into distinct compatible atoms.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:cfree}, every admissible next atom has a new $\ell$.  For a
fixed preceding compatible set, (39.14), the residue-profile upper bound
(39.7), and (39.15) bound the sum over all possible next atoms by
\begin{equation}\tag{39.19}
 Ct^2\sum_{\substack{k\leq K\\k\equiv1(4)}}
 \frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^3}q_y(k)
 \prod_{\substack{p\mid(k,R)\\p>y}}\frac p{p-1}
 \leq Ct^2\log K\leq Ct^3.
\end{equation}
Removing the forbidden old $\ell$'s only decreases this sum.  Iteration
proves (39.18).
\end{proof}

At pair level, for fixed $k,k'$ and $g=(k,k')$,
\begin{equation}\tag{39.20}
 \sum_{A:k_A=k}\sum_{\substack{B:k_B=k'\\A\sim B}}
 \Pr(E_A\cap E_B)
 \ll\frac g{\varphi(g)}W_kW_{k'}.
\end{equation}
Averaging (39.20) uses
$\alpha_p^2(p/(p-1)-1)=O(p^{-3})$, not
$p\alpha_p^2\asymp1/p$.  Hence the pair excess is $O(\mu_X^2)$ with an
absolute constant.  It is \emph{not} $o(\mu_X)$: a fixed small prime already
contributes a constant multiple of $\mu_X^2$ to the usual Janson dependency
sum.  Therefore the proposed direct condition $\Delta=o(\mu)$ and the naive
Janson proof fail quantitatively.  The factorial-moment theorem survives
because an absolute base $C^m$, rather than Poisson relative error, is enough
after the separate void estimate below.

\subsection{The CRT void without partitioning by all of $M_0$}
A completely unconditioned $c$-free void still has a quarantine floor:
taking $n\equiv0\pmod p$ for many small $p$ disables every atom whose
multiplier contains one of them.  Thus $Q_r(H_X)$ alone cannot have cubic
mean.  Take
\begin{equation}\tag{39.21}
 y=Bt^3
\end{equation}
with a sufficiently large fixed $B$, and multiply by the exact small-prime
coprimality selector
\begin{equation}\tag{39.22}
 S_y(n)=\mathbf1_{(n,P_y)=1}
 =\sum_{d\mid P_y}\mu(d)\mathbf1_{d\mid n}.
\end{equation}
Every exceptional prime greater than $y$ has $S_y=1$.  Notice that (39.22)
has degree $\pi(y)=O(t^3/\log t)$, not the impossible degree $\pi(K)$.

\begin{lemma}[source Lemma 39.5: conditioned $c$-free void; proved,
provisional]\label{lem:conditioned-void}
In the exact CRT space,
\begin{equation}\tag{39.23}
 \Pr(H_X=0\mid S_y=1)\leq e^{-ct^3}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Reveal all multiplier coordinates.  Let $c$ denote the resulting class
modulo $24L_K$, where
$L_K=\lcm\{k\leq K:k\equiv1\pmod4\}$, and put
\begin{equation}\tag{39.24}
 \mathcal J_c=\{k\leq K:k\equiv1\pmod4,\ (k,c)=1\},
 \qquad
 Z(c)=\sum_{\substack{y<p\leq K,\ p\mid L_K\\p\mid c}}\frac1p.
\end{equation}
The restriction $p\mid L_K$ makes $Z$ well defined: a prime
$p\equiv3\pmod4$ with $3p>K$ divides no admissible multiplier, so it has
no CRT coordinate here and can be omitted; it removes nothing from
$\mathcal J_c$.  The coordinates for $p>y$, $p\mid L_K$, remain independent
and $\Pr(p\mid c)=1/p$.  With exponential parameter $y$,
\begin{equation}\tag{39.25}
 \mathbb E e^{yZ}
 \leq\exp\left\{Cy\sum_{p>y}p^{-2}\right\}=e^{O(1)},
 \qquad \Pr(Z>\eta)\leq e^{-\eta y+O(1)}.
\end{equation}

Write $h(\mathcal J)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal J}\varphi(k)/k^2$.  The elementary
bounds
\begin{equation}\tag{39.26}
 h(\mathcal K(K))\asymp\log K,
 \qquad
 \sum_{\substack{k\leq K\\p\mid k}}\frac{\varphi(k)}{k^2}
 \ll\frac{\log K}{p}
\end{equation}
show by the union bound that, for a sufficiently small fixed $\eta$,
$Z(c)\leq\eta$ implies
$h(\mathcal J_c)\geq c_1\log K$.  Also $S_y=1$ makes $c$ coprime to $24$,
and by definition it is coprime to $L_{\mathcal J_c}$.

Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}, the source-notes Theorem 34.8, to this
arbitrary subfamily $\mathcal J_c$.  Its lower proof lies in the canonical
boxes (39.2), and its good triples are contained in the $c$-free unpruned
family.  Conditional on the multiplier coordinates, the distinct
$\ell$-coordinates are independent by Lemma~\ref{lem:cfree}.  Therefore,
when $Z(c)\leq\eta$,
\begin{equation}\tag{39.27}
 \Pr(H_X=0\mid c)
 \leq\prod_\ell\left(1-\frac{f_c^{\rm good}(\ell)}\ell\right)
 \leq\exp\{-c_2t^2h(\mathcal J_c)\}
 \leq e^{-c_3t^3}.
\end{equation}
The bad multiplier coordinates cost at most
$e^{-\eta Bt^3+O(1)}$ by (39.25).  Increasing $B$ if necessary and averaging
(39.27) proves (39.23).
\end{proof}

This is the load-bearing use of Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.  It replaces
Janson by supplying cubic active prime-coordinate mass uniformly for every
reduced surviving multiplier fibre.  The only fibres not reduced are those
which set too much large-prime multiplier mass to zero, and (39.25) makes
those fibres exponentially rare after the polynomial-sized coprimality
cutoff.  The dependency on that provisional theorem is therefore explicit
and unavoidable in the present argument.

\subsection{A low-degree majorant with the honest ledger}
For even $r$, put
\[
 Q_r(h)=\sum_{j=0}^r(-1)^j\binom hj.
\]
The exact identity
\begin{equation}\tag{39.28}
 Q_r(0)=1,\qquad Q_r(h)=\binom{h-1}r\geq0\quad(h\geq1)
\end{equation}
shows that
\begin{equation}\tag{39.29}
 \nu_X(n)=S_y(n)Q_r(H_X(n))
\end{equation}
is nonnegative and is at least one on every exceptional prime
$n>\max(K,y)$.

\begin{theorem}[source Theorem 39.6: modified $c$-free critical-window
assembly; proved, \textsc{claimed/provisional}]\label{thm:cfree-assembly}
There are constants $c,C_0,D_B>0$, depending at most on $\kappa$, such that
if $r$ is the least even integer at least $D_Bt^3$, then (39.29) has exact
CRT mean
\begin{equation}\tag{39.30}
 \mathbb E_{\rm CRT}\nu_X\leq e^{-ct^3}.
\end{equation}
Here and below $D_B$ is the Bonferroni-degree constant, distinct from the
source Lemma 16.2's $\omega$-cutoff constant $D$ in (39.3).  Its congruence
expansion has the explicit bounds
\begin{equation}\tag{39.31}
 \begin{split}
 \text{degree}&\leq r+\pi(y)=O(t^3),\\
 \log d_{\rm term}&\leq O(y)+r(t+\log K)=O(t^4),\\
 \log\sum_{\rm terms}|c_{\rm term}|&=O(rt)=O(t^4).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
Every nonzero term is one plain congruence class.  Consequently, whenever
$\log N\geq C_0t^4$,
\begin{equation}\tag{39.32}
 \sum_{n\leq N}\nu_X(n)\ll Ne^{-ct^3}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
From (39.28), for $h\geq1$,
$\binom{h-1}r\leq\binom h{r+1}$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:conditioned-void} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:factorial-cfree} therefore give
\begin{equation}\tag{39.33}
 \begin{split}
 \mathbb E(Q_r(H_X)\mid S_y=1)
 &\leq\Pr(H_X=0\mid S_y=1)
       +\frac{\mathbb E((H_X)_{r+1}\mid S_y=1)}{(r+1)!}\\
 &\leq e^{-c_1t^3}+\frac{(Ct^3)^{r+1}}{(r+1)!}
 \leq e^{-ct^3},
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
when $D_B$ is sufficiently large.  Multiplication by
$\Pr(S_y=1)\leq1$ proves (39.30).

Expand (39.22) and the elementary symmetric polynomials in $Q_r$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:cfree} makes every compatible atom set use distinct
$\ell$'s; CRT then makes its further intersection with $d\mid P_y$ either
empty or one class.  If $A_X=|\mathcal A_X|$, the crude choice count from
(39.2) gives $\log A_X=O(t)$, and hence
\begin{equation}\tag{39.34}
 2^{\pi(y)}\sum_{j\leq r}\binom{A_X}j=\exp\{O(rt)\}.
\end{equation}
The modulus is at most $P_y(KX)^r$, proving (39.31).  On $[1,N]$, each
class count is its CRT mean times $N$, plus $O(1)$.  Choosing $C_0$ larger
than the constants in (39.31) makes the aggregate rounding error
$\exp\{O(rt)\}$ at most, say, $N^{1/2}$, which is negligible beside
$Ne^{-ct^3}$.  This proves (39.32).
\end{proof}

The literal source hypothesis $H_{\rm PF}'(k\ell;{\rm good})$ demanded
coefficient sum $e^{O(J)}$, with $J\asymp t^3$, and a majorant for its larger
aggregate avoider predicate.  Theorem~\ref{thm:cfree-assembly} proves
\emph{neither} clause: its direct even-Bonferroni expansion has ledger
$e^{O(Jt)}$, and it only covers the $c$-free avoider, hence all exceptional
primes.  These are deliberate modifications, not suppressed losses.  The
ledger still closes because the critical window itself has logarithmic size
$\asymp Jt$.  Thus the literal $e^{O(J)}$ clause remains \textsc{open} as
stated, but is no longer needed for the exceptional set if
Theorem~\ref{thm:cfree-assembly} survives review.

\subsection{Exceptional denominators}
\begin{theorem}[source Theorem 39.7: unconditional $3/4$ exceptional-set
bound; \textsc{claimed/provisional}]\label{thm:three-quarter}
There is a constant $c>0$ such that
\begin{equation}\tag{39.35}
 E_{\rm all}(N)\ll N\exp\{-c(\log N)^{3/4}\}.
\end{equation}
The same bound holds for exceptional primes.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Internal argument; provisional status retained]
Put $L=\log N$, take $t=\alpha L^{1/4}$, and set $X=e^t$.  Choose the fixed
$\alpha>0$ small enough that $L\geq C_0t^4$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:cfree} and (39.29) majorize every exceptional prime larger
than $\max(K,y)$; that omitted range is negligible.  Equations (39.32) and
$t^3=\alpha^3L^{3/4}$ give the prime bound.

For completeness, we spell out the semigroup transfer used for all
denominators.  Every prime factor of an exceptional integer $n>1$ is itself
exceptional: if $p\mid n$ and
$4/p=1/x_1+1/x_2+1/x_3$, scaling the three denominators by $n/p$ represents
$4/n$.  Thus the exceptional integers are contained in the multiplicative
semigroup generated by exceptional primes, including the empty product.
The integer $1$ contributes only $O(1)$.

Write $g(u)=u^{3/4}$ and $x=e^L$.  Fix $x_0$ large enough that the prime
bound gives, for every $x\geq x_0$,
\[
 E_{\rm prime}(x)\ll x\exp\{-c_0g(\log x)\}.
\]
Fix $0<\eta<c_0$ and, for large $L$, put
$\delta=\eta g(L)/L$, so $0<\delta<1/4$.  Rankin's inequality over the
semigroup gives
\[
 E_{\rm all}(x)\leq x^{1-\delta}
 \prod_{\substack{p\leq x\\p\ \text{exceptional}}}
       (1-p^{-1+\delta})^{-1}.
\]
The finitely many exceptional primes $p\leq x_0$ contribute $O(1)$,
uniformly in $\delta$.  Partial summation and the endpoint estimate
$\delta L-c_0g(L)=-(c_0-\eta)g(L)<0$ give
\[
 \sum_{\substack{p\leq x\\p\ \text{exceptional}}}p^{-1+\delta}
 \ll1+\int_{\log x_0}^L\exp\{\delta u-c_0g(u)\}\,du.
\]
Because $g(u)/u=u^{-1/4}$ is decreasing, for $u\leq L$ one has
$\delta u\leq\eta g(u)$.  The last integral is therefore bounded by
$\int_{\log x_0}^\infty e^{-(c_0-\eta)g(u)}\,du<\infty$.  The prime-power
terms in the logarithm of the Euler product also satisfy
\[
 \sum_{p\leq x}\sum_{j\geq2}\frac{p^{-j(1-\delta)}}j
 \leq\sum_p\sum_{j\geq2}\frac{p^{-3j/4}}j=O(1).
\]
Hence the Euler product is $O(1)$ and
$E_{\rm all}(x)\ll x^{1-\delta}=x\exp\{-\eta g(\log x)\}$, proving
(39.35).
\end{proof}

\begin{center}
\fbox{\parbox{0.91\linewidth}{\centering
Theorem~\ref{thm:three-quarter} is \textsc{claimed/provisional}.  It uses
the likewise provisional Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.  It does not use the
source hypotheses $H_{k\rm BV}$, $H_{\rm PF}'$, or Elliott--Halberstam, but
``unconditional'' does not remove the need for external verification.}}
\end{center}

\subsection{Remarks on verification status}\label{sec:verification-remarks}
The three weakest steps identified by the maximum-severity self-review are
reproduced faithfully here.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
\item The new residue-resolved upper bound (39.11) must be rederived by a
referee with every local factor present.  It needs simultaneous uniformity
in $k\leq X^\kappa$, every divisor $g\mid k$, and every unit residue; the
proof uses Brun--Titchmarsh for the upper bound, so it does not hide a
polynomially small Bombieri--Vinogradov main term.  This is the most
load-bearing new analytic lemma.
\item Lemma~\ref{lem:conditioned-void} uses Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} for the
data-dependent subfamily $\mathcal J_c$ and uses the canonical lower boxes,
not merely the larger family in the statement of the source formula
(34.18).  The quantifiers of Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} do allow every
subfamily containing $1$, and its proof furnishes the lower mass in those
boxes, but this inheritance is new and needs an independent line-by-line
check together with Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} itself.
\item The ordered-moment induction (39.14)--(39.19) is where lcm collapse
and residue consistency cancel at every prime power.  A missed factor there
would be exponentiated through $r\asymp t^3$.  The displayed conditional
factor is $\varphi(p^e)$ for $p\leq y$ and $p^e$ for $p>y$; after the
$1/\varphi(g)$ residue share, only $p/(p-1)$ remains at an unconditioned
shared prime.  This exact replay, including unequal prime-power exponents,
is mandatory in external review.
\end{enumerate}

Internally, the quantifier chain is complete: Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} is
uniform in $\mathcal J_c$; (39.25) removes the nonreduced fibres; (39.18)
controls the even Bonferroni tail; and (39.31)--(39.34) pay the full
$e^{O(t^4)}$ coefficient ledger before selecting
$t=\alpha(\log N)^{1/4}$.  The direct Janson proposal is rejected because
its dependency sum is $\Theta(\mu^2)$, while the original $e^{O(J)}$
formulation remains open.  Theorem~\ref{thm:three-quarter} should not be
cited as established outside this campaign until an independent expert has
checked the three steps above and the priority claim against the primary
literature.

A fresh search found no post-1970 improvement of Vaughan's
$N/\exp(c(\log N)^{2/3})$ exceptional-set bound.  Pomerance--Weingartner's
2025 preprint \cite[\S4]{PomeranceWeingartner} explicitly cites Vaughan
1970 as the state of the art and reproduces the same $2/3$ exponent when
its general theorem is specialized to $m=4$.  Access to Vaughan's paper was
blocked, but DOI \href{https://doi.org/10.1112/S0025579300002886}
{10.1112/S0025579300002886} was confirmed.  If
Theorem~\ref{thm:three-quarter} survives external review, it would therefore
be the first exponent improvement found since 1970.  This is a search
result, not a literature guarantee; the \textsc{claimed/provisional} label
stands.

\subsection{Internal verification pedigree}\label{sec:pedigree}
The counts in this subsection concern internal checks, not independent
external validation.  Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} has had three independent
internal checks.  In the third, a fresh reviewer rederived the power-sized
extension of the box argument, expanded the second incidence moment, paid
all boundary errors, and rebuilt the low-congestion
Bombieri--Vinogradov assembly.  The reviewer checked Shiu's 1980 paper
\cite{Shiu} directly: the interval and modulus inequalities hold with
Shiu's parameters $\alpha=\beta=1/4$, the residue classes are reduced, and
both multiplicative-function hypotheses apply in the fixed-relative-length
boxes with $H=K^{10}$.  The use of $1\in\mathcal J$, same-prime
distinctness, the canonical lower boxes, and the data-dependent family
$\mathcal J_c$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:conditioned-void} were separately replayed.
That check was \textsc{confirmed-after-repairs}; the provisional register
did not change.

The downstream fixed-$c$-free chain has, in addition to its original
construction, one attested-blind parallel construction and two paper
fidelity passes.  For the blind construction, the unit read only the task
brief and the source-note analogues of Sections~16 and~34 at repository
commit \texttt{c489912b}, and froze its complete S1--S7 derivation at
commit \texttt{347a570} before opening the source analogue of Section~39.
It did not inspect the earlier verification program, although the mandatory
baseline run printed that program's summary line.  Git verifies the
snapshots, texts, and commit order; it cannot verify what was visible on
screen or enforce read isolation.  The blindness claim is therefore an

classified all seven steps as \textsc{confirmed-same-method}; the variants
(modulus $k$ versus $\lcm(g,\rad k)$ in Shiu, a pointwise Euler bound, and
direct weighted-fibre averaging) made no quantifier or range change.  Its

are bug hunts, not asymptotic evidence, and the blind unit treated

Wave 15 then ran three separate maximum-severity hostile reviews, one each

\section{General numerators}\label{sec:general-m}
For $m\geq3$, define
\begin{equation}\tag{43.1}
 E_m(N)=\#\{n\leq N:m/n\text{ is not a sum of three positive unit
 fractions}\}.
\end{equation}
This concerns the rational number: reducing a common factor does not change
representability.  We nevertheless count the unreduced pairs, as does
Pomerance--Weingartner \cite{PomeranceWeingartner}.

\subsection{The identity and the first exceptional-set layer}
\begin{lemma}[general-numerator multiplier identity]\label{lem:m-identity}
% (wave-16 fidelity repair) Restore the positivity hypotheses from notes Lemma 43.1.
Let $k,\ell\geq1$, suppose $k\ell\equiv-1\pmod m$, and let
$A=(k\ell+1)/m=uvw$ be a factorization into positive integers.  If $n>0$, $nv\equiv-u\pmod{k\ell}$, and
$s=(nv+u)/(k\ell)$, then
\begin{equation}\tag{43.2}
 \frac mn=\frac1{suw}+\frac1{nsvw}+\frac1{nuvw}.
\end{equation}
Moreover $(A,k\ell)=1$, so $v$ is invertible modulo $k\ell$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The congruence makes $s$ a positive integer.  The numerator over the common
denominator $nsuvw$ is
$nv+u+s=s(k\ell+1)=smuvw$.  A common divisor of $A$ and $k\ell$ divides
$mA-k\ell=1$.
\end{proof}
No parity, primality, or coprimality condition on $m$ and $n$ occurs here.
For a prime $\ell\nmid m$, the old condition $k\equiv1\pmod4$ is replaced
by $k\equiv-\ell^{-1}\pmod m$.

Put
\begin{equation}\tag{43.3}
 \eta_1(m)=\prod_{p\mid m}\frac p{p+1},\qquad
 \eta_2(m)=\prod_{p\mid m}\frac {p^2}{p^2+p-1},\qquad
 C_2=\prod_p(1-p^{-1})\left(1+\frac{p-1}{p^2}\right).
\end{equation}
\begin{lemma}[exact thinning factors]\label{lem:m-thinning}
For fixed $m$ and every reduced $r\pmod m$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.4}
 \begin{split}
 \sum_{\substack{k\leq K\\k\equiv r\ (m)}}\frac{\varphi(k)}{k^2}
 &\sim\frac{\eta_1(m)}{\varphi(m)\zeta(2)}\log K,\\
 \sum_{\substack{k\leq K\\k\equiv r\ (m)}}
       \frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^3}
 &\sim\frac{C_2\eta_2(m)}{\varphi(m)}\log K.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
If $\log m=O(\log K)$, the aggregate sums over $(k,m)=1$ are, up to
constants depending only on that $O$-constant,
$\eta_1(m)\log K$ and $\eta_2(m)\log K$.  Also
\begin{equation}\tag{43.5}
 \frac{\varphi(m)}m\leq\eta_1(m)\leq
 \zeta(2)\frac{\varphi(m)}m,
 \qquad \eta_2(m)\asymp\frac{\varphi(m)}m.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The Euler series for $\varphi(k)/k$ is
$\zeta(s+1)/\zeta(s+2)$; removing multiples of $p\mid m$ deletes the
local factor $1+1/p$.  For $b(k)=(\varphi(k)/k)^2$, the local harmonic
factor is
\[
 1+\sum_{e\geq1}\frac{(1-p^{-1})^2}{p^e}
 =1+\frac{p-1}{p^2}.
\]
Character orthogonality divides the principal pole by $\varphi(m)$ and,
for fixed $m$, each nonprincipal twist is regular there.  The same
convolution argument without characters proves the uniform aggregate
bounds.  Finally, divide each product in (43.3) by $\varphi(m)/m$; the
resulting Euler products converge absolutely.
\end{proof}
The fixed-class asymptotics in (43.4) are deliberately not asserted
uniformly for growing $m$.  In particular, no Siegel-uniform estimate and no
equidistribution among short residue classes is used below.  Only the
aggregate nonnegative family $(k,m)=1$ is used.

Define the multiplicative function
\[
 F_m(p^a)=\begin{cases}1,&p\mid m,\\p/(p-1),&p\nmid m.
 \end{cases}
\]
For $(u,v)=1$ one has the exact identity
\begin{equation}\tag{43.6}
 \frac1{\varphi(muv)}=\frac{F_m(u)F_m(v)}{\varphi(m)uv}.
\end{equation}
This avoids both a false extra factor $1/m$ and the false restriction
$(uv,m)=1$.

\begin{lemma}[general-$m$ class mass]\label{lem:m-class-mass}
Fix $B>0$.  Let $K_0\leq K\leq(\log X)^B$, $H=K^{10}$,
$m\leq(\log X)^B$, and
$\mathcal J\subseteq\{k\leq K:(k,m)=1\}$ contain $1$.  Put
$h(\mathcal J)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal J}\varphi(k)/k^2$ and suppose
$(c,L_{\mathcal J})=1$.  % (wave-16 fidelity repair) Restore the prime range from notes Lemma 43.3.
For primes $X^{1/2}<\ell\leq X$, let $f_{m,c}(\ell)$ count the distinct residues
$-uv^{-1}\pmod\ell$ furnished by
\begin{equation}\tag{43.7}
 \begin{gathered}
 k\in\mathcal J,\\ H<u,v\leq\ell^{1/3},\quad
 (u,v)=(uv,k)=1,\quad \omega(uv)\leq D\log\log X,\\
 muv\mid k\ell+1,
 \qquad k\mid u+cv.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Then, with constants depending only on $B$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.8}
 \sum_{X^{1/2}<\ell\leq X}\frac{f_{m,c}(\ell)}\ell
 \asymp_B\frac{(\log X)^2h(\mathcal J)}{\varphi(m)}.
\end{equation}
Each counted residue, combined with the congruence modulo $k$, is forced by
Lemma~\ref{lem:m-identity}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For fixed $k$, the lattice congruence is still $u+cv\equiv0\pmod k$.
Thus the box count, boundary ratio $O(K^2/H)$, low-$\omega$ Rankin tail,
and Shiu modulus condition $k<U^{1/2},V^{1/2}$ are unchanged.  The
condition $k\equiv-\ell^{-1}\pmod m$ is not a second lattice condition:
after $(k,u,v)$ is fixed, all congruences are carried by the one reduced
prime progression
\begin{equation}\tag{43.9}
 \ell\equiv-k^{-1}\pmod{muv}.
\end{equation}
The modulus is the product $muv$, not $\lcm(m,uv)$; common prime powers,
including powers of $2$, are already present with their summed exponent.
In the canonical boxes $uv\leq x^{1/3}$, so a fixed log-power $m$ remains
below the Bombieri--Vinogradov level.  Each modulus has at most
$K2^{\omega(uv)}$ descriptions, absorbed by choosing the logarithmic saving
in terms of $B$.  Brun--Titchmarsh gives the upper half.  Identity (43.6)
and the same two-variable harmonic calculation give exactly the factor
$1/\varphi(m)$ on both sides.  Shiu's auxiliary modulus remains $k$; no
condition $(uv,m)=1$ is inserted.

For deduplication, a collision at one $\ell$ gives
$\ell\mid uv'-u'v$ and $|uv'-u'v|<\ell^{2/3}<\ell$, hence reducedness gives
$(u,v)=(u',v')$.  The two atoms then fix $k\pmod{muv}$, and
$muv>mH^2>K$ gives $k=k'$.  Finally $n\equiv c\pmod{L_{\mathcal J}}$ and
a hit modulo $\ell$ combine to $nv\equiv-u\pmod{k\ell}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[first general-numerator layer; \textsc{claimed/provisional}]
\label{thm:general-two-third}
For every fixed $m\geq3$ there is an absolute $c_0>0$ such that, for all
sufficiently large $N$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.10}
 E_m(N)\ll_m N\exp\left\{-c_0
 \left(\frac{\eta_1(m)}{\varphi(m)}\right)^{1/3}
 (\log N)^{2/3}(\log\log N)^{1/3}\right\}.
\end{equation}
The prime-denominator and all-denominator forms are both uniform, for every
fixed $\eps>0$, when $3\leq m\leq(\log N)^{2-\eps}$, after an
$\eps$-dependent lower threshold.  This theorem has a complete internal
proof but remains provisional externally because it would improve the
published general-$m$ benchmark.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Internal argument; provisional status retained]
Take $K=\lfloor\delta\log N\rfloor$, all $k\leq K$ coprime to $m$, and
$M_0=\lcm_{k\leq K,(k,m)=1}k$.  Since
$\log M_0\leq(1+o(1))K$, each reduced subsequence has length
$N^{1-O(\delta)}$.  Apart from the $O(\log m)$ primes dividing $m$, every
prime denominator above $K$ occurs in one.  Lemmas~\ref{lem:m-thinning}
and~\ref{lem:m-class-mass} give uniformly in its residue $c$ the prime
class mass
\[
 \asymp\lambda_m(\log X)^2\log K,
 \qquad \lambda_m=\frac{\eta_1(m)}{\varphi(m)}.
\]
The upper mass has the same factor, so the larger-sieve Rankin truncation
used in Theorem~\ref{thm:record} closes with
\[
 \log X=\alpha\left(\frac{\log N}{\lambda_m\log\log N}\right)^{1/3}.
\]
This gives (43.10) for primes.  In the stated range,
$\log X=o(\log N)$ and $K,m$ are fixed powers of $\log X$; the sieve
length and BV/Shiu levels follow with a saving chosen in terms of $\eps$,
not $m$.

If $a\mid n$ and $m/a$ is representable, scaling its denominators by $n/a$
represents $m/n$.  Thus every divisor, in particular every prime factor, of
an exceptional $n$ is exceptional.  The semigroup argument in Theorem~\ref{thm:three-quarter} applies with
$g_m(u)=\lambda_m^{1/3}u^{2/3}(\log u)^{1/3}$.  For the uniform transfer put
$\Theta_1=\varphi(m)/\eta_1(m)\asymp m$, fix the final margin
$0<\eps_0<2$, choose $0<\eps'<\eps_0/(3-\eps_0)$, and start the prime
estimate at $\log x_0\asymp\Theta_1^{1/(2-\eps')}$.  Its Rankin parameter
is $\delta\asymp\Theta_1^{-1/3}(\log N)^{-1/3}
(\log\log N)^{1/3}$, and for $\Theta_1\ll(\log N)^{2-\eps_0}$,
\[
 \delta\log x_0\ll(\log N)^{-\gamma}(\log\log N)^{1/3}=o(1),
 \qquad
 \gamma=\frac{\eps_0(1+\eps')-3\eps'}{3(2-\eps')}>0.
\]
Declaring every smaller prime exceptional costs
$\exp\{O(\log\Theta_1)\}$, absorbed by the displayed saving.  Above $x_0$,
$g_m(u)/u$ decreases and $g_m(\log x_0)$ is a positive power of
$\Theta_1$, so partial summation is uniform.  This gives the same
$3\leq m\leq(\log N)^{2-\eps_0}$ range for all denominators.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The provisional cubic transfer}
Fix $0<\kappa<1/240$, put $t=\log X$, $K=\lfloor X^\kappa\rfloor$,
$H=K^{10}$, and use the dyadic blocks $x<\ell\leq2x$ in
$(X^{1/2},X]$.  The general-$m$ $c$-free atoms are
\begin{equation}\tag{43.11}
 \begin{gathered}
 A=(k,\ell,u,v),\quad k\leq K,\quad(k,m)=1,
 \quad H<u,v\leq x^{1/6},\\
 (u,v)=(uv,k)=1,
 \quad\omega(uv)\leq D\log\log X,
 \quad muv\mid k\ell+1,\\
 E_A=\{n:n\equiv-uv^{-1}\pmod{k\ell}\}.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:cfree}, with $4uv$ replaced by $muv$, shows
that the coupling $k\mid u+cv$ is automatic after revealing
$c\equiv n\pmod{L_{m,K}}$, distinct compatible atoms have distinct
$\ell$'s, and
\begin{equation}\tag{43.12}
 \Pr\left(\bigcap_{i\leq j}E_{A_i}\right)
 =\frac{\mathbf1_{\rm compatible}}
 {\lcm(k_1,\ldots,k_j)\prod_i\ell_i}.
\end{equation}
The two relevant inequalities only improve: $x^{1/3}<\ell$ makes $(u,v)$
unique, and $muv>K$ then makes $k$ unique.

\begin{lemma}[general-$m$ mass profiles]\label{lem:m-profiles}
Uniformly for $m\leq t^B$, $g\mid k$, and reduced $a\pmod g$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.13}
 W_{k,a}(g)\ll_B\frac{t^2}{\varphi(m)\varphi(g)}
                  \frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^3},
 \qquad
 W_k\asymp_B\frac{t^2}{\varphi(m)}\frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^3}.
\end{equation}
Consequently
\begin{equation}\tag{43.14}
 \mu_{m,X}\asymp_B\frac{\eta_2(m)}{\varphi(m)}t^3.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Identity (43.6) extracts $1/\varphi(m)$.  In boxes put
$q_0=\lcm(g,\rad k)$.  Since $(k,m)=1$, there are still
$\varphi(q_0)^2/\varphi(g)$ compatible reduced class pairs.  Shiu applies
to $F_m\leq n/\varphi(n)$ because $q_0\leq k<U^{1/10},V^{1/10}$, and gives
\[
 \sum_{\rm boxes}\frac{F_m(u)F_m(v)}{uv}
 \ll\frac{t^2}{\varphi(g)}\left(\frac{\varphi(k)}k\right)^2.
\]
Brun--Titchmarsh on modulus $muv$ proves the upper profile.  For the BV
lower profile, replacing $n/\varphi(n)$ by $F_m(n)$ changes the coprime-pair
factor at $p\mid m$ by
$(p^2-1)/(p^2+1)$; the product is bounded away from zero.  The Rankin
truncation and allocation multiplicity are unchanged.  Summation and the
second aggregate formula in Lemma~\ref{lem:m-thinning} prove (43.14).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[general-$m$ pruned cubic slice; \textsc{claimed/provisional}]
\label{thm:m-pruned}
For fixed $B$, uniformly for $m\leq t^B$, every
$\mathcal J\subseteq\{k\leq K:(k,m)=1\}$ containing $1$, and every reduced
fibre $(c,L_{\mathcal J})=1$, the restriction
$r_{\mathcal J}(u,v;c)\leq t^4$ leaves
\begin{equation}\tag{43.15}
 \sum_{X^{1/2}<\ell\leq X}\frac{f^{\rm good}_{m,c}(\ell)}\ell
 \asymp_B\frac{t^2h(\mathcal J)}{\varphi(m)}.
\end{equation}
For the full coprime family this is
$\asymp\eta_1(m)t^3/\varphi(m)$.  This theorem inherits the provisional
status of Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof ledger; provisional status retained]
The incidence second moment is unchanged after restricting the outer
$k$-set to $(k,m)=1$: it sees only $k\mid u+cv$, and its lcm boundary is
at most $K^2$.  Markov pruning therefore removes
$o(t^2h(\mathcal J))$.  Identity (43.6) leaves at least
$1/\varphi(m)$ of the retained harmonic mass.  The retained multiplicity is
$2^{\omega(uv)}t^4$, while BV runs over $muv\leq t^Bx^{1/3}$; increasing
its fixed logarithmic saving by $B$ makes the error
$o(x\log x\,h(\mathcal J)/\varphi(m))$.  Deduplication is (43.12), and the
upper bound is Lemma~\ref{lem:m-profiles}.  These are all entries at which
$m$ changes the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.
\end{proof}

For completeness, the prime-power moment cancellation also survives.
Condition on $(n,P_y)=1$ and add a compatible atom with multiplier $k$ to a
set whose multiplier lcm is $R$.  With $g=(k,R)$, the exact density ratio is
$q_y(k)b_y(g)/(k\ell)$, while compatibility fixes one unit residue modulo
$g$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:m-profiles} supplies $1/\varphi(g)$, leaving
\begin{equation}\tag{43.16}
 \frac{b_y(g)}{\varphi(g)}
 =\prod_{p\mid g,\ p>y}\frac p{p-1}.
\end{equation}
Primes dividing $m$ do not divide $k$.  At an unmodified such prime, deleting
the multiplier Euler factor gives $p^2/(p^2+p-1)$; if $p\leq y$, it instead
gives the smaller $p/(p+1)$.  Thus the total deletion factor is at most
$\eta_2(m)$, and
\begin{equation}\tag{43.17}
 \sum_{\substack{k\leq K\\(k,m)=1}}
 \frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^3}q_y(k)
 \prod_{p\mid(k,R),\ p>y}\frac p{p-1}
 \ll\eta_2(m)\log K.
\end{equation}
The ordered induction, including unequal prime-power exponents, gives
\begin{equation}\tag{43.18}
 \mathbb E(H_{m,X})_j\leq
 \left(C\frac{\eta_2(m)}{\varphi(m)}t^3\right)^j
 \leq(Ct^3)^j.
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[source Theorem 43.8; \textsc{claimed/provisional}]
\label{thm:general-three-quarter-conservative}
For every fixed $m\geq3$, and uniformly for each fixed $\eps>0$ in
$3\leq m\leq(\log N)^{3/4-\eps}$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.28}
 E_m(N)\ll N\exp\left\{-c_0\frac{\eta_1(m)}{\varphi(m)}
                         (\log N)^{3/4}\right\}.
\end{equation}
This holds for primes and all denominators and inherits the provisional
status of Theorems~\ref{thm:pruned} and~\ref{thm:three-quarter}.
\end{theorem}
It is the direct \S39 replay with $y,r\asymp t^3$; it remains valid, but is
superseded by the thinned-window theorem below.

\subsection{Pomerance--Weingartner comparison}
Pomerance--Weingartner's Theorem~1.3 \cite{PomeranceWeingartner} states that
there is an absolute $C>0$ such that, for $4\leq m\leq(\log N)^2$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.32}
 E_m(N)\leq N\exp\left\{-C
       \left(\frac{(\log N)^2}{\varphi(m)}\right)^{1/3}\right\}.
\end{equation}
Their prime mass is $(\log X)^2/\varphi(m)$, so no
$(\log\log N)^{1/3}$ is hidden in (43.32).  For the optimized provisional
layer, write $L=\log N$ and compare the exponent scales
\begin{equation}\tag{43.33}
 R=\left(\frac{\eta_2(m)}{\varphi(m)}\right)^{1/4}L^{3/4},
 \qquad P=\frac{L^{2/3}}{\varphi(m)^{1/3}}.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\tag{43.34}
 \frac RP=\{\eta_2(m)^3\varphi(m)L\}^{1/12},
 \qquad R\geq P\ \Longleftrightarrow\
 L\geq\frac1{\eta_2(m)^3\varphi(m)}.
\end{equation}
Unknown absolute constants prevent reading (43.34) as a literal finite
threshold.  In the common stated domain $4\leq m\leq L^2$, the formal new
scale is larger once (43.34) holds, and it eventually does for each fixed
$m$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:general-three-quarter} continues through every
fixed-gap range $m\leq L^{3-\eps}$, where the quoted published theorem is
not stated once $m>L^2$; at $m\asymp L^3$ the new saving stops growing, so
no claim is made there or beyond.  This scale comparison does not erase the
provisional-versus-published evidentiary distinction.  The first layer is
uniform for primes and all denominators through $m\leq L^{2-\eps}$ and has
exponent ratio $(\eta_1(m)\log L)^{1/3}$ relative to (43.32).

\subsection{The attested-blind thinned window}
The protocol and adjudication are recorded in
Subsection~\ref{sec:pedigree}.  In particular, the blind construction's
finite relative-mass inequality was adjudicated line by line, but neither
that internal verdict nor the attestation upgrades the inherited status.

\begin{theorem}[source Theorem 43.12; \textsc{claimed/provisional}]
\label{thm:general-three-quarter}
Put $L=\log N$.  For every fixed $\eps>0$, uniformly for
$3\leq m\leq L^{3-\eps}$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.35}
 E_m(N)\ll_\eps N\exp\left\{-c_\eps
 \left(\frac{\eta_2(m)L^3}{\varphi(m)}\right)^{1/4}\right\}.
\end{equation}
The same formula holds for exceptional primes.  It inherits the provisional
Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} through Theorem~\ref{thm:m-pruned}, together with
every maximum-severity qualification in
Subsection~\ref{sec:verification-remarks} (source \S39.7); the internal
wave-16 adjudication changes the exponent and range, not the external
status.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Internal argument; provisional status retained]
Put
\[
 \theta_m=\frac{\eta_2(m)}{\varphi(m)},\qquad
 \Theta_m=\theta_m^{-1}\asymp m,\qquad M=\theta_mt^3.
\]
The shrinking-window input is a finite relative-mass inequality.  If
$H_m(K)=\sum_{k\leq K,(k,m)=1}\varphi(k)/k^2$, then for every prime
$p\nmid m$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.36}
 \begin{split}
 \sum_{\substack{k\leq K,(k,m)=1\\p\mid k}}\frac{\varphi(k)}{k^2}
 &=\sum_{e\geq1}\frac{1-p^{-1}}{p^e}
   \sum_{\substack{a\leq K/p^e\\(a,pm)=1}}
        \frac{\varphi(a)}{a^2}
 \leq\frac{H_m(K)}p.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
For $p\mid m$ the left side is zero.  Reveal the multiplier coordinates,
put $\mathcal J_c=\{k\leq K:(k,m)=(k,c)=1\}$, and set
$Z(c)=\sum_{y<p\leq K,\ p\mid c,\ p\mid L_{m,K}}1/p$.  The union bound
and (43.36) give
$h(\mathcal J_c)\geq H_m(K)(1-Z(c))$.  Independence above $y$ gives
\begin{equation}\tag{43.37}
 \mathbb E\e^{yZ}\leq
 \exp\left\{Cy\sum_{p>y}p^{-2}\right\}=\e^{O(1)},
 \qquad \Pr\{Z>\eta_0\}\leq\e^{-\eta_0y+O(1)}
\end{equation}
for a fixed $0<\eta_0<1$.

Take an integer $y\asymp B_0M$, choosing the absolute $B_0$ after the
good-fibre constant.  On $Z\leq\eta_0$,
Theorem~\ref{thm:m-pruned} gives active prime-coordinate mass
\[
 \frac{t^2h(\mathcal J_c)}{\varphi(m)}
 \gg\frac{\eta_1(m)}{\varphi(m)}t^3\asymp M,
\]
so the conditioned void is $\e^{-cM}$; (43.37) makes the bad-fibre cost the
same size.  The estimate is uniform because
$y\sum_{p>y}p^{-2}=O(1)$ and $\eta_1\asymp\eta_2$.
Equation (43.18) supplies every factorial moment with base $CM$.  Taking
$r$ to be the least even integer at least $D_0M$, with fixed sufficiently
large $D_0$, Stirling and the exact even-Bonferroni identity give
\begin{equation}\tag{43.38}
 \mathbb E_{\rm CRT}\{S_yQ_r(H_{m,X})\}
 \leq\e^{-cM}+\frac{(CM)^{r+1}}{(r+1)!}\leq\e^{-c'M}.
\end{equation}

The resulting expansion has the genuinely thinned ledger
\begin{equation}\tag{43.39}
 \begin{aligned}
 \deg&=O(M),\\
 \log d_{\rm term}&\leq O(y)+r(t+\log K)=O(Mt),\\
 \log\sum|c_{\rm term}|&=O(rt)=O(Mt).
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Here $\log|\mathcal A_{m,X}|=O(t)$, and the atom modulus is $k\ell$, not
$mk\ell$: $m$ selects atoms but contributes no ledger factor.  Thus
finite-interval rounding is absorbed when
\begin{equation}\tag{43.40}
                         L\geq C_0Mt=C_0\theta_mt^4.
\end{equation}
Choose $t=\alpha(L/\theta_m)^{1/4}$ with fixed sufficiently small $\alpha$.
Then
\begin{equation}\tag{43.41}
                         M\asymp\theta_m^{1/4}L^{3/4},
\end{equation}
which proves (43.35) for primes.

The fixed-gap range supplies every analytic constraint.  Since
$\Theta_m\asymp m\leq L^{3-\eps}$,
$t/L\asymp(\Theta_m/L^3)^{1/4}\ll L^{-\eps/4}$ and
$M\gg L^{\eps/4}$.  Also $m\leq t^4$ after a harmless fixed enlargement,
$K=\e^{\kappa t}\gg m$, $muv$ remains exponentially below the
Bombieri--Vinogradov level, $2\leq y<X^{1/2}$, and
$\max(m,K,y)=\e^{o(L)}$.  Shrinking $y$ exposes more primes, but
(43.16)--(43.18) already sum their local factors uniformly.

For the all-denominator transfer fix a final margin $0<\eps_0<3$, choose
$0<\eps'<\eps_0/(4-\eps_0)$, and start the prime theorem at
$u_0=\log x_0\asymp\Theta_m^{1/(3-\eps')}$.  With
$g_m(u)=\Theta_m^{-1/4}u^{3/4}$ and
$\delta\asymp\Theta_m^{-1/4}L^{-1/4}$,
\begin{equation}\tag{43.42}
 \delta u_0\ll L^{-\gamma},\qquad
 \gamma=\frac{\eps_0(1+\eps')-4\eps'}{4(3-\eps')}>0.
\end{equation}
Thus declaring all smaller primes exceptional costs only
$\exp\{O(\log\Theta_m)\}=\e^{o(g_m(L))}$.  Above $x_0$,
$g_m(u)/u$ decreases and
$g_m(u_0)=\Theta_m^{\eps'/(4(3-\eps'))}$ beats the partial-summation
Jacobian uniformly.  The semigroup argument of
Theorem~\ref{thm:three-quarter} preserves the exponent and the full
$L^{3-\eps_0}$ fixed-gap range.
\end{proof}
No pointwise equidistribution among the classes $k\pmod m$ is used, and no
growing-saving claim is made at $m\asymp L^3$ or beyond.

\section{Pointwise Type-I slices}\label{sec:slices}
\subsection{Exact vanishing and finite conspiracies}
For an odd prime $p$, put
\[
 \mathcal B_p=\left\{(c,k):1\leq k\leq\lfloor2p/3\rfloor,
  \ 1\leq c\leq\left\lfloor\frac{2p+k}{4k}\right\rfloor,
  \ (p,ck)=1\right\}.
\]
For $(c,k)\in\mathcal B_p$, let $h=4ck$,
$N_{c,k}=p^2+4ck^2$, and define the raw ordered Type-I slice
\begin{equation}\tag{44.1}
 M_{c,k}(p)=\#\{D:D\mid N_{c,k},\ D\equiv-p\pmod h\}.
\end{equation}
Each such divisor gives the ordered row
$a=(D+p)/h$, $b=(N_{c,k}/D+p)/h$ in
$p(a+b)=k(4abc-1)$, and every row arises this way.

If $N_{c,k}=\prod_j\ell_j^{e_j}$, character orthogonality gives the exact
vanishing law
\begin{equation}\tag{44.2}
 \begin{split}
 M_{c,k}(p)
 &=\frac1{\varphi(h)}\sum_{\chi\ ({\rm mod}\ h)}
   \overline{\chi(-p)}
   \prod_j(1+\chi(\ell_j)+\cdots+\chi(\ell_j)^{e_j}),\\
 M_{c,k}(p)=0
 &\Longleftrightarrow
 \nexists(0\leq u_j\leq e_j):
       \prod_j\ell_j^{u_j}\equiv-p\pmod{4ck}.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
The second line is an exponent-box condition, not merely subgroup
membership; vanishing need not be caused by one character factor.  This is
the raw count.  The primitive count additionally requires $(a,b)=1$ and can
vanish when the raw count does not: for $(p,c,k)=(241,7,3)$ the two target
divisors give $(a,b)=(3,66),(66,3)$, so $M_{7,3}(241)=2$ but the primitive
mass is zero.

The deterministic obstruction is a moving genus character.  Write
$c=st^2$ with $s$ squarefree, let $\Delta_s<0$ be the fundamental
discriminant of $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-s})$, and put
\begin{equation}\tag{48.1}
                         \chi_s(n)=\left(\frac{\Delta_s}{n}\right).
\end{equation}
Its conductor divides $4s$.
\begin{theorem}[moving genus obstruction; source Theorem 48.1]
\label{thm:moving-genus}
Let $p\equiv1\pmod {24}$ and $(c,k)\in\mathcal B_p$.  Then
\begin{equation}\tag{48.2}
                  \chi_s(p)=1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad M_{c,k}(p)=0.
\end{equation}
The restriction of $\chi_s$ to the hard progression is identically one if
and only if
\begin{equation}\tag{48.3}
                         s\in\{1,2,3,6\}.
\end{equation}
For every other squarefree $s$, the hard primes with $\chi_s(p)=1$ have
relative Dirichlet density $1/2$, apart from the finitely many ramified
primes; admissibility for fixed $(c,k)$ removes only the additional finite
set dividing $ck$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Every prime $\ell\mid p^2+4s(tk)^2$ is odd and prime to $stk$, and
$(p(2tk)^{-1})^2\equiv-s\pmod\ell$.  Hence $\chi_s(\ell)=1$, and every
divisor $D$ of the norm has $\chi_s(D)=1$.  Since the conductor divides
$4s\mid4ck$, a target divisor would instead satisfy
$\chi_s(D)=\chi_s(-p)=-\chi_s(p)$, proving (48.2).  The primitive
quadratic character is constant on the kernel of reduction modulo $24$
exactly when its conductor divides $24$.  The negative fundamental
discriminants with that property are $-4,-8,-3,-24$, which give precisely
(48.3).  Otherwise the restriction is a nonprincipal quadratic character,
so half of the compatible reduced classes have value one; Dirichlet's
theorem gives the density assertion.
\end{proof}
Thus all eight slices $ck\leq4$ and the $80$ slices $ck\leq30$ with
$s\in\{1,2,3,6\}$ vanish on every hard prime.  For each of the other $31$
slices, Theorem~\ref{thm:moving-genus} still forces a positive-density
vanishing subfamily, but it is only a sufficient source of zeros.

The remaining slices can conspire beyond fixed congruence data:
$193\equiv1033\pmod{840}$, both the $(5,1)$ and $(7,1)$ slices vanish at
$193$, but the $(5,1)$ slice is positive at $1033$.  Conversely, every
prime $p\equiv97\pmod{120}$ has a positive $(5,1)$ slice via $D=3$; this is
the already known multiplier progression, not a new supply.

An exact finite census over the $385$ primes $p<30000$,
$p\equiv1\pmod{24}$, gives
\[
\begin{array}{c|rrrrrrr}
 C&4&5&10&15&20&25&30\\ \hline
 \#\{(c,k):ck\leq C\}&8&10&27&45&66&87&111\\
 \#\{p:\text{all slices vanish}\}&385&220&161&63&41&30&15.
\end{array}
\]
This is a finite exact factorization census, not an asymptotic theorem.  The
$15$ primes for which all $111$ slices vanish include $2521$; its known
twelve ordered Type-I rows all lie outside $ck\leq30$.

\subsection{Fixed divisors, residue-one escape, and depth}
The positive progression just displayed is one instance of an exact law.
\begin{theorem}[fixed-divisor progression law; source Theorem 48.4]
\label{thm:fixed-divisor-slice}
Fix $(c,k)$, put $h=4ck$, and fix a positive integer $d$ prime to $h$.
Let $L_d=\lcm(24,h,d)$.  A reduced class $r\pmod {L_d}$ is a hard-prime
class on which this fixed $d$ guarantees $M_{c,k}(p)>0$ if and only if
\begin{equation}\tag{48.16}
 r\equiv1\pmod {24},\qquad r\equiv-d\pmod h,
 \qquad r^2\equiv-4ck^2\pmod d.
\end{equation}
Every prime in such a class for which $(c,k)\in\mathcal B_p$ has
\begin{equation}\tag{48.17}
 e=\frac{p^2+4ck^2}{d},\qquad a=\frac{p+d}{h},\qquad
 b=\frac{p+e}{h},\qquad p(a+b)=k(4abc-1).
\end{equation}
Within the proof shape ``one specified divisor has the target grade,'' these
conditions are also necessary; classes modulo multiples of $L_d$ are
exactly refinements of them.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The last congruence in (48.16) is $d\mid p^2+4ck^2$, and the middle one is
$d\equiv-p\pmod h$.  Conversely those two facts give a target divisor in
(44.1).  Since the norm is $p^2\pmod h$, its cofactor $e$ has the same
target grade, so (48.17) is integral; direct expansion proves its final
identity.  The first congruence is exactly the hard-prime restriction.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[residue-one escape; source Theorem 48.5]
\label{thm:slice-residue-one}
No class in (48.16) contains $1\pmod {L_d}$.  Consequently, for every
$Q$, the union of all fixed-divisor slice guarantees with $L_d\leq Q$
misses the infinite prime class
\begin{equation}\tag{48.20}
 p\equiv1\pmod {\Lambda_Q},\qquad
 \Lambda_Q=\lcm\{24,L_d:L_d\leq Q\text{ occurs above}\}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
If residue one were guaranteed, then $d\equiv-1\pmod h$ and
$d\mid1+4ck^2=1+hk$.  The cofactor $e=(1+hk)/d$ would also be
$-1\pmod h$, so $d,e\geq h-1$, whereas
\[
 (h-1)^2-(1+hk)=h(h-k-2)>0,
 \qquad h-k-2=k(4c-1)-2\geq1,
\]
a contradiction.  There are finitely many triples with $L_d\leq Q$, and
Dirichlet's theorem supplies infinitely many primes in (48.20).
\end{proof}
This is the slice analogue of the residue-one compactness wall for the
paper's fixed identity classes.  It does not control a divisor chosen from
the moving factorization of each prime, and ``not guaranteed'' does not mean
``vanishing.''  The later Theorem~\ref{thm:slice-lower} instead quantifies
the related residue-one \emph{genus} construction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-core}; it proves actual slice vanishing through a
logarithmic box, not a consequence of this fixed-divisor non-guarantee.

For scale only, let $ck_{\min}(p)$ be the first positive admissible slice
whose squarefree $c$-core is not in $\{1,2,3,6\}$ and put
$D(p)=ck_{\min}(p)-1$.  The exact streamed census finds
$ck_{\min}(p)\leq77$ for all $385$ hard primes below $30000$, with maximum
$D=76$ at $p=12289$; the $15$ full-$ck\leq30$ conspiracies have first
positive product between $31$ and $77$.  The optional census finds
$ck_{\min}(p)\leq103$ for all $1181$ hard primes below $10^5$, with maximum
$D=102$ at $p=92401$.  These are finite computations, not a growth law or
pointwise progress.  Proving $ck_{\min}(p)<\infty$ for every hard prime
would already prove the Type-I form of Erd\H{o}s--Straus and hence the
conjecture; it remains open.

\section{Conditional pointwise routes through the slice frame}
\label{sec:conditional-slices}
\medskip\noindent\textbf{Scope and verdict.}
This section isolates a one-prime-factor event sufficient for a Type-I
solution and states the corresponding conditional theorem.  Its hypothesis
is deliberately labelled \textsc{bespoke and unproved}: none of
Bateman--Horn, Linnik, GRH for Dirichlet, Hecke, or ray-class $L$-functions,
Elliott--Halberstam, or Duke-type equidistribution has this pointwise
factorization conclusion in its standard form.  The audit makes the
quantifier failure exact.  Two proved walls sharpen the earlier slice
analysis: prime values of the norm have the wrong grade, and any bounded
collection of squarefree cores is simultaneously genus-forced on infinitely
many hard primes.  This section's deliverable is a rigorous conditional
reduction and a map of its missing pointwise input.  The later stacked sieve,
Theorem~\ref{thm:growing-stack}, proves an almost-all form but still does not
settle that pointwise quantifier or claim that a standard conjecture secretly
settles Erd\H{o}s--Straus.

\subsection{The good prime-factor class and exact reduction}
Fix a hard prime $p$, an admissible slice $(c,k)\in\mathcal B_p$, and write
\begin{equation}\tag{50.1}
 c=st^2,\quad s=\operatorname {sf}(c),\qquad
 h=4ck,\qquad N=N_{c,k}=p^2+4ck^2.
\end{equation}
Inflate the primitive genus character $\chi_s$ to
$(\mathbb Z/h\mathbb Z)^\times$; its conductor divides $4s\mid h$.  Put
\begin{equation}\tag{50.2}
 K_s(h)=\ker\chi_s,
 \qquad \mathcal G_p(c,k)=\{-p\pmod h\}\cap K_s(h).
\end{equation}
Every prime $q\mid N$ lies in $K_s(h)$, while
\begin{equation}\tag{50.3}
 \mathcal G_p(c,k)=
 \begin{cases}
   \{-p\pmod h\},&\chi_s(p)=-1,\\
   \varnothing,&\chi_s(p)=1,
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
because $\chi_s(-1)=-1$.  Thus the moving-genus theorem is exactly the
empty-good-set case.  On an unforced slice there is still only one good ray
grade, not half the factor grades; a split prime is the wrong kind of
divisor unless it is also $-p\pmod h$.

\begin{theorem}[one-prime-factor slice criterion; source Theorem 50.1]
\label{thm:one-prime-slice}
Let $p$ be an odd prime and $(c,k)\in\mathcal B_p$.  If a prime $q$
satisfies
\begin{equation}\tag{50.4}
 q\mid p^2+4ck^2,
 \qquad q\pmod {4ck}\in\mathcal G_p(c,k),
\end{equation}
then the $(c,k)$-slice is positive and gives a Type-I Erd\H{o}s--Straus
representation.  Explicitly, with
\begin{equation}\tag{50.5}
 e=\frac{p^2+4ck^2}{q},\qquad
 a=\frac{p+q}{4ck},\qquad b=\frac{p+e}{4ck},
\end{equation}
one has $a,b\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$ and
\begin{equation}\tag{50.6}
 p(a+b)=k(4abc-1),\qquad
 \frac4p=\frac1{ack}+\frac1{bck}+\frac1{pabc}.
\end{equation}
Primality of $q$ does not imply $(a,b)=1$, and no primitivity is needed.
For example, $q=11$ at $(p,c,k)=(241,7,3)$ gives $(a,b)=(3,66)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Admissibility gives $(p,ck)=1$, hence $(N,h)=1$ and $(q,h)=1$.  The target
grade is $q\equiv-p\pmod h$, while $N\equiv p^2\pmod h$; therefore
$e=Nq^{-1}\equiv-p\pmod h$.  This proves the integrality and positivity
in (50.5).  Substitution of $q=ha-p$, $e=hb-p$, and $qe=N$, followed by
cancellation of $p^2$, gives $4ck^2=h^2ab-hp(a+b)$.  Division by
$h=4ck$ gives the first identity in (50.6), and the unit-fraction identity
follows over the common denominator $pabck$.  Reduction of the first
identity modulo $p$ gives $4abc\equiv1\pmod p$, so exactly the denominator
$pabc$ is divisible by $p$.  This is Type I.
\end{proof}
The raw/primitive boundary is intentional: the selected prime is a raw
target divisor, and a nonprimitive row still proves the displayed identity.
The canonical decomposition can recover a primitive Type-I tuple if wanted.

\begin{hypothesis}[slice prime factor $H_{\rm SPF}(A)$; bespoke and unproved]
\label{hyp:SPF}
Fix $A>0$.  There is a constant $p_0=p_0(A)$ such that every prime
$p>p_0$, $p\equiv1\pmod {24}$, admits positive integers $c,k$ and a prime
$q$ with
\begin{equation}\tag{50.7}
 \boxed{\quad ck\leq(\log p)^A,\qquad
 q\mid p^2+4ck^2,\qquad q\equiv-p\pmod {4ck}.\quad}
\end{equation}
\end{hypothesis}
% wave-18 fidelity repair: restore the source genus-automaticity caveat.
This is a quantitative sufficient hypothesis, not the logically weakest
one-prime-factor statement: deleting the log-power bound is weaker.  It
puts no bound on $q$, prescribes no slice, imposes no primitivity, and needs
no extra genus condition, which is automatic from (50.3).  It is stronger
than slice positivity, since a product of wrong-grade prime factors can hit
the target even when no one prime does.  Theorem~\ref{thm:slice-lower}
proves that $H_{\rm SPF}(A)$ is false for every $0<A<1$; exactly the
exponents $A\geq1$ remain open.

% wave-18 fidelity repair: preserve the source theorem's unproved-hypothesis label.
\begin{theorem}[conditional on $H_{\rm SPF}(A)$; $H_{\rm SPF}(A)$ unproved;
source Theorem 50.2]
\label{thm:SPF}
If $H_{\rm SPF}(A)$ holds for some $A$, then every sufficiently large hard
prime has a Type-I Erd\H{o}s--Straus representation.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For sufficiently large $p$, (50.7) gives $ck<p$, $3k\leq2p$, and
$4ck\leq2p+k$.  Hence $(p,ck)=1$ and $(c,k)\in\mathcal B_p$.  The
congruence in (50.7) makes $q$ the unique good class in (50.2), so
Theorem~\ref{thm:one-prime-slice} applies.
\end{proof}
If $q$ is fixed first, (50.4) is exactly the pair of root progressions
\begin{equation}\tag{50.8}
 p\equiv-q\pmod {4ck},\qquad
 p\equiv\pm2k\sqrt{-c}\pmod q,
 \qquad p\equiv1\pmod {24}.
\end{equation}
The fixed-divisor law preassigns $q$ and obtains a fixed CRT class; (50.7)
asks the actual factorization attached to each $p$ to supply some $q$ after
$p$ is known.  The residue-one escape walls bounded collections of the
former, not the latter.

\subsection{Prime values, bounded cores, and least nonresidues}
\begin{lemma}[prime norms are wrong-grade; source Lemma 50.3]
\label{lem:prime-norm}
For a hard prime $p$ and any admissible $(c,k)$, if $N_{c,k}$ is prime then
the slice vanishes.  More generally, a good prime factor in (50.4) is
$3\pmod4$, its complementary divisor is also $3\pmod4$, and hence
$N_{c,k}$ must be composite.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The target grade is $-p\equiv3\pmod4$, whereas
$N_{c,k}\equiv1\pmod4$.  If $N$ is prime, both divisors $1,N$ have grade
$1\pmod4$.  Under (50.4), both $q$ and $N/q$ have the target grade by
Theorem~\ref{thm:one-prime-slice}.
\end{proof}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Assessment 50.4 (Bateman--Horn).}
Standard Bateman--Horn gives an asymptotic in the variable for a fixed
finite list of irreducible integer polynomials.  It neither controls every
external coefficient $p$ uniformly down to a $(\log p)^A$ interval nor
prescribes a prime factor of a composite value.  Applied literally to
$p^2+4ck^2$ as a prime-value polynomial, success triggers
Lemma~\ref{lem:prime-norm}, not (50.7).  A uniform assertion that every $p$
has a logarithmic-box value with a factor in the moving grade
$-p\pmod {4ck}$ would imply Theorem~\ref{thm:SPF}, but merely restates
$H_{\rm SPF}$; it is not standard Bateman--Horn.  The same quantifier defect
applies to Hardy--Littlewood prime tuples.  Standard smooth-value
conjectures control factor sizes, not the bounded exponent box of grades in
$(\mathbb Z/h\mathbb Z)^\times$.

\begin{theorem}[bounded-core genus escape; source Theorem 50.5]
\label{thm:bounded-core}
For every fixed $B$, infinitely many hard primes satisfy
\begin{equation}\tag{50.9}
 \chi_s(p)=1\quad\hbox{for every squarefree }s\leq B.
\end{equation}
For each such prime, every admissible slice whose squarefree $c$-core is at
most $B$ vanishes, for all $k$.  Consequently no absolute bound for a least
active core, and no proof using only boundedly many cores with arbitrary
$k$, can hold.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let
$R_B=24\prod_{\substack{\ell\leq B\\ \ell\text{ prime}}}\ell$.
Dirichlet's theorem gives infinitely many primes $p\equiv1\pmod {R_B}$.
For every odd prime $\ell\leq B$, quadratic reciprocity and
$p\equiv1\pmod4$ give $(\ell/p)=(p/\ell)=1$; the factor $24$ gives
$(2/p)=(-1/p)=1$.  Multiplicativity proves (50.9), and
Theorem~\ref{thm:moving-genus} makes every asserted slice zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[GRH gives an active slice only; source Theorem 50.6]
\label{thm:GRH-active}
Conditional on the unproved Generalized Riemann Hypothesis, every
sufficiently large hard prime $p$ has a squarefree
$s\ll(\log p)^2$ with $\chi_s(p)=-1$; hence $(c,k)=(s,1)$ is an admissible
unforced slice in a logarithmic box.  This does \emph{not} assert that the
slice is positive.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The standard Ankeny consequence of GRH gives an integer
$n\ll(\log p)^2$ with $(n/p)=-1$.  Its squarefree kernel $s$ has the same
Legendre symbol and is no larger.  Since $p\equiv1\pmod8$,
$\chi_s(p)=(-s/p)=(s/p)=-1$.  Admissibility is automatic for large $p$;
the residual exponent-box condition in (44.2) remains untouched.
\end{proof}
Thus least-nonresidue technology supplies permission for a slice, not the
divisor in it.  Linnik similarly finds a small prime in
$q\equiv-p\pmod h$ but gives no reason for that prime to divide the fixed
integer $N_{c,k}$.  Asking for both properties is a bespoke form of (50.7),
not an existing Linnik hypothesis.

\subsection{The GRH/Chebotarev compositum wall}
With the notation (50.1), put
\begin{equation}\tag{50.10}
 K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-s}),\qquad
 \alpha=p+2tk\sqrt{-s},\qquad
 N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\alpha)=N_{c,k}.
\end{equation}
For a prime $q\nmid2ck$,
\begin{equation}\tag{50.11}
 q\mid N_{c,k}
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \text{some }\mathfrak q\mid q\text{ in }K\text{ divides }(\alpha)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 p\equiv\pm2k\sqrt{-c}\pmod q.
\end{equation}
In particular $q$ splits in $K$, but the converse is much weaker: (50.11)
selects a prime-ideal divisor of the one specified principal ideal
$(\alpha)$.  Ring-class Chebotarev controls primes represented by form
classes, not the factorization of a fixed represented integer.  Combining
(50.11) with the ray condition gives (50.8), a condition on $p$ modulo $q$
when $q$ is fixed, not a Frobenius condition on $p$ in one fixed field as
$q$ varies.  Effective Chebotarev under GRH does not turn the infinite
disjunction over actual divisors of $(\alpha)$ into a pointwise
factorization theorem.

A quantitative check exposes the scale.  Restrict $ck\leq L$ and fixed
candidate primes $q\leq Z$.  Before deduplication, (50.8) supplies at most
two classes modulo $4ckq$, so
\begin{equation}\tag{50.12}
 \begin{split}
 \mu_{\rm raw}(L,Z)
 &\leq\frac12\sum_{ck\leq L}\frac1{ck}
       \sum_{\substack{q\leq Z\\q\equiv3\ (4)\\q\text{ prime}}}\frac1q\\
 &=\left(\frac18+o(1)\right)(\log L)^2\log\log Z.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
This is a class-mass envelope, not a coverage theorem.  Although there are
$L\log L+O(L)$ slices in the box, all $k$ over one core share one genus bit.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Assessment 50.7 (the compositum wall).}
A literal joint-Chebotarev implementation introduces cyclotomic conductors
containing $\lcm(1,\ldots,4L)$ and candidate primes through $Z$; its naive
compositum degree is exponential on the $L+Z$ scale.  The same
GRH-effective bookkeeping as the earlier Chebotarev audit limits a direct
compositum to $L+Z=O(\log N)$ when $p\leq N$ is counted.  At
$L,Z\asymp\log N$, even an ideal independent-events model applied to
(50.12) predicts only
\begin{equation}\tag{50.13}
 \exp\{-O((\log\log N)^2\log\log\log N)\}
\end{equation}
for the survivor proportion.  Allowing an average sieve to take
$Z=N^\theta$ changes the model exponent only to $O((\log\log N)^3)$.
Both leave $N^{1-o(1)}$ possible survivors and are much weaker in shape
than the \textsc{claimed/provisional} bound
$N\exp\{-c(\log N)^{3/4}\}$.  GRH supplies no independence theorem for the
overlapping root classes, so (50.13) is an audit ceiling, not a conditional
exceptional-set theorem.  There is no GRH exceptional-set improvement to
record here.  Elliott--Halberstam and Kloosterman-refined levels of
distribution change average ranges but retain an exceptional set and do not
imply (50.7) in standard form.

\subsection{The exact genus pairing and the Duke wall}
For a product cutoff $L$, the exact active-box mass is
\begin{equation}\tag{50.14}
 T_L^-(p)=
 \sum_{\substack{ck\leq L,\ (c,k)\in\mathcal B_p\\
                   \chi_{\operatorname {sf}(c)}(p)=-1}}
 \frac1{\varphi(4ck)}\sum_{\chi\ ({\rm mod}\ 4ck)}
 \overline{\chi(-p)}
 \prod_{\ell^e\parallel p^2+4ck^2}
       (1+\chi(\ell)+\cdots+\chi(\ell)^e).
\end{equation}
Inflate $\chi_s$ to modulus $h$.  Every factor $\ell\mid N$ has
$\chi_s(\ell)=1$, while an active slice has $\chi_s(-p)=1$.  Therefore
\begin{equation}\tag{50.15}
 \text{the terms indexed by }\chi
 \text{ and }\chi\chi_s\text{ are equal}.
\end{equation}
For a genus-forced slice they are opposites and cancel.  Restricting to
active slices removes that one cancellation but leaves every character of
the residual quotient of $K_s(h)$; it does not make the principal character
dominant.  The principal/genus pair contributes
\begin{equation}\tag{50.16}
 P_L^-(p)=
 \sum_{\substack{ck\leq L,\ (c,k)\in\mathcal B_p\\
                   \chi_{\operatorname {sf}(c)}(p)=-1}}
 \frac{2\tau(p^2+4ck^2)}{\varphi(4ck)}
 \leq p^{o(1)}(\log L)^2\log\log(3L)
\end{equation}
when $L=p^{o(1)}$.  It is positive when the active box is nonempty, but the
remaining ray characters can cancel it exactly.  At $p=2521$, for example,
active cores $11,17,19,22,23,29$ occur in $ck\leq30$, yet every unforced
slice in that box vanishes.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Assessment 50.8 (Duke/class-group GRH).}
Summing (50.14) does not create a fixed theta series: the discriminant,
ray modulus, norm, and active-core selection all move.  The
Hurwitz--Kronecker identity restores class numbers only after removing the
ray projector and changing to a weighted all-divisor mass.  Equation
(50.15) removes exactly the genus character and no other part of that
projector.  GRH for class-group or ray-class $L$-functions controls prime
or ideal sums, not the target coefficient of the finite Euler product of
one norm.  A Duke-type theorem would therefore need a new uniform
statement for the whole moving family (50.14), with error $o(P_L^-)$; this
is a genuinely new hypothesis, not a recognized consequence of GRH for the
relevant $L$-functions.

\subsection{Conditional frontier and census}
The audit is summarized by
\begin{equation}\tag{50.17}
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{input}&\text{standard output}&\text{missing condition}\\ \hline
% wave-18 fidelity repair: restore the source (50.17) Bateman--Horn and Linnik caveats.
\text{Bateman--Horn}&\text{prime polynomial values on average in the variable}
 &\text{a composite value with a good factor, uniformly per }p\\
\text{Linnik}&\text{a prime }q\equiv-p\pmod h&q\mid N_{c,k}\\
\text{GRH/Chebotarev}&\text{split/ray primes in fixed fields}
 &\mathfrak q\mid(\alpha)\text{ for each fixed }\alpha\\
\text{least nonresidue under GRH}&\chi_s(p)=-1
 &\text{the residual exponent-box hit}\\
\text{EH/GEH/Kloosterman}&\text{average distribution in }p
 &\text{an empty exceptional set}\\
\text{Duke/class-group GRH}&\text{unprojected or averaged form mass}
 &\text{the moving ray projector}
\end{array}
\end{equation}
The proved obstructions are Lemma~\ref{lem:prime-norm},
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-core}, the fixed-divisor escape
Theorem~\ref{thm:slice-residue-one}, and the exact pairing (50.15).  The
claims about named hypotheses are assessments of their standard
conclusions, not logical independence theorems.  Within the pointwise
frontier (50.7), Theorem~\ref{thm:slice-lower} refutes every $0<A<1$ and
leaves exactly $A\geq1$ open, while Corollary~\ref{cor:almost-all-SPF}
proves that the failure set is triple-logarithmically small.  Replacing ``prime factor'' by
``divisor'' returns the open unrestricted depth criterion.

For finite comparison define
\begin{equation}\tag{50.18}
 ck_{\rm pr}(p)=\min\{ck:(c,k)\in\mathcal B_p,
 \ \exists\hbox{ prime }q\mid p^2+4ck^2,
 \ q\equiv-p\pmod {4ck}\}.
\end{equation}
\medskip\noindent\textbf{Computational 50.9 (exact stated range;
informational).}
A streamed exact computation finds $ck_{\rm pr}(p)$ for all $385$ hard
primes below $30000$, checks the complete exponent box at every tested norm,
and reconstructs (50.5)--(50.6) for each good-prime event.  Its distribution
is
\begin{equation}\tag{50.19}
\begin{array}{c|rrrrrrrrrrr}
ck_{\rm pr}&5&7&10&11&13&14&17&19&21&22&23\\
\#&156&30&36&35&13&15&16&4&15&9&6\\ \hline
ck_{\rm pr}&26&28&29&31&33&34&35&37&38&39&42\\
\#&10&4&3&1&2&3&1&1&4&2&5\\ \hline
ck_{\rm pr}&43&46&55&62&66&67&69&70&77&78&\\
\#&1&1&2&1&4&1&1&1&1&1&
\end{array}
\end{equation}
and the strict records are
\begin{equation}\tag{50.20}
\begin{array}{c|rrrrrrrr}
p&73&193&241&1201&2521&4729&7489&9601\\
ck_{\rm pr}&7&10&21&34&38&66&70&78.
\end{array}
\end{equation}
The prime-factor minimum equals the unrestricted $ck_{\min}$ for $311$
primes and is larger for $74$.  The largest gap is $55$ at $p=23689$:
$ck_{\min}=11$ but $ck_{\rm pr}=66$, witnessed by
$(c,k,q)=(33,2,77951)$.  The largest $ck_{\rm pr}$ is $78$ at $p=9601$.
The optional memory-bounded scan over the $1181$ hard primes below $10^5$
has maximum $282$ at $p=83689$.  These observations support no pointwise
growth law; the later almost-all theorem does not use them.  The verification
factors one norm at a time and stores at most one residue set of size $4ck$.

\begingroup
\setlength{\abovedisplayskip}{6pt plus 2pt minus 2pt}
\setlength{\belowdisplayskip}{6pt plus 2pt minus 2pt}
\setlength{\abovedisplayshortskip}{2pt plus 1pt}
\setlength{\belowdisplayshortskip}{4pt plus 1pt minus 1pt}
\linespread{0.97}\selectfont
\section{Witness-modulus tails and the effectivity repair}
\label{sec:witness-tail}
For a prime, or any positive integer, $p$, define
\begin{equation}\tag{51.1}
 W(p)=W_{\rm II}(p)=\min k\ell,
\end{equation}
where the minimum is over positive $k,\ell,u,v,w$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\tag{51.2}
 k\ell\equiv3\pmod4,\qquad \frac{k\ell+1}{4}=uvw,
 \qquad p\equiv-uv^{-1}\pmod {k\ell}.
\end{equation}
Put $W(p)=+\infty$ if there is no such tuple; no general finiteness
assertion is made.  Lemma~\ref{lem:multiplier} says exactly that $W(p)\leq T$
supplies an explicit representation with multiplier modulus at most $T$.
The definition allows composite $\ell$, although the proof uses a prime
subfamily.

\subsection{Parameterized truncation and the master tail}
Write $t=\log X$, let $K\geq K_0$, and suppose that the canonical dyadic
boxes (39.2)--(39.3) furnish, uniformly in every
$\mathcal J\subseteq\{k\leq K:k\equiv1\pmod4\}$ containing $1$ and every
reduced compatible fibre $c$,
\begin{equation}\tag{51.3}
 \sum_{X^{1/2}<\ell\leq X}\frac{f_{c;\mathcal J}(\ell)}\ell
 \asymp t^2h(\mathcal J),\qquad
 h(\mathcal J)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal J}\frac{\varphi(k)}{k^2}.
\end{equation}
Put
\begin{equation}\tag{51.4}
                         \mu=t^2\log K.
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[truncated $c$-free assembly; proved internally, with the
provisional review status of the fixed-$c$-free chain]
\label{lem:truncated-cfree}
Assume (51.3), $KX\leq T$, $\log K\ll t$, and
\begin{equation}\tag{51.5}
                         \log N\geq C\mu t.
\end{equation}
Then, with effective constants whenever the supply constants in (51.3) are
effective,
\begin{equation}\tag{51.6}
 \#\{p\leq N:p\text{ prime},\ W(p)>T\}
       \ll N\exp\{-c\mu\}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use the atoms (39.2)--(39.4), all of modulus at most $KX\leq T$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:cfree}'s geometry needs only $u,v\leq X^{1/6}$ and
$4uv>4H^2>K$.  The profile and exact CRT/Euler induction replay as
\begin{equation}\tag{51.7--51.8}
 W_{k,a}(g)\ll\frac{t^2}{\varphi(g)}\frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^3},
 \qquad \mathbb E(H_X)_m\leq(C\mu)^m.
\end{equation}
for every needed order.  With $y=B\mu$, the bad fibres cost
$\exp\{-\eta B\mu+O(1)\}$, while the others have
$h(\mathcal J_c)\gg\log K$; (51.3) therefore gives
$\Pr(H_X=0\mid S_y=1)\leq e^{-c\mu}$.  The fixed-polylog lower proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:prime-harvest} and the cubic proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} both supply exactly these canonical dyadic boxes.

Take the least even $r\geq D_B\mu$.  The same Bonferroni factorial tail has
CRT mean $O(e^{-c\mu})$ and ledger
\begin{equation}\tag{51.11}
 \deg=O(\mu),\qquad \log d_{\rm term}=O(\mu t),\qquad
 \log\sum|c_{\rm term}|=O(\mu t).
\end{equation}
Since $y<X^{1/2}$ and the atom count has logarithm $O(t)$, (51.5) absorbs
rounding and the primes at most $\max(K,y)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[master witness-modulus tail; proved internally with the
exact status below]\label{thm:witness-tail}
There are effectively computable positive constants such that the following
hold.  Every set counted with variable $p$ in this theorem and
Corollary~\ref{cor:witness-polylog} is restricted to primes.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Fixed-polylogarithmic supply, without
Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.}  Uniformly when
\begin{equation}\tag{51.12}
 3\leq T,\qquad
 \log N\geq C\{1+(\log T)^3\log(2+\log T)\},
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}\tag{51.13}
 \#\{p\leq N:W(p)>T\}
 \ll N\exp\{-c(\log T)^2\log(2+\log T)\}.
\end{equation}
This is unconditional in the no-unproved-hypothesis sense and does not
inherit Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}; it does inherit the campaign's
provisional-review qualification on the Section~\ref{sec:cfree} moment and
Bonferroni machinery.
\item \textbf{Cubic supply.}  Fix $0<\kappa<1/240$.  Uniformly when
\begin{equation}\tag{51.14}
 3\leq T,\qquad \log N\geq C_\kappa\{1+(\log T)^4\},
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}\tag{51.15}
 \#\{p\leq N:W(p)>T\}
 \ll_\kappa N\exp\{-c_\kappa(\log T)^3\}.
\end{equation}
This statement inherits Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} and is
\textsc{claimed/provisional}.
\end{enumerate}
Both estimates are uniform throughout the simpler common range
\begin{equation}\tag{51.16}
                    3\leq T\leq\exp\{c(\log N)^{1/4}\}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For (1), set $X=T^{1/2}$ and
$K=\lfloor(\log X)^5\rfloor$, with fixed adjustments below the asymptotic
threshold.  Then $KX\leq T$, $\log K\asymp\log t$, and the canonical-box
proof of \eqref{eq:mult-mass} supplies (51.3).  Thus
$\mu\asymp t^2\log t$ and (51.5) is (51.12).  For (2), use the same $X$
and $K=\lfloor X^\kappa\rfloor$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} supplies (51.3),
$\mu\asymp t^3$, and (51.5) becomes (51.14).  Bounded $T$ is absorbed by
enlarging the implied constants.
\end{proof}
At $T=\exp\{\alpha(\log N)^{1/4}\}$, (51.15) is
$N\exp\{-c(\log N)^{3/4}\}$, exactly the prime part of (39.35) up to
constants.  This is a consistency check, not a new record.

\begin{corollary}[polylogarithmic tails; same status as
Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-tail}]\label{cor:witness-polylog}
For each fixed $A>0$,
\begin{equation}\tag{51.17}
 \#\{p\leq N:W(p)>(\log N)^A\}
 \ll_A N\exp\{-c_A(\log\log N)^2\log\log\log N\},
\end{equation}
unconditionally and without Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.  The cubic supply
gives the additional \textsc{claimed/provisional} estimate
\begin{equation}\tag{51.18}
 \#\{p\leq N:W(p)>(\log N)^A\}
 \ll_A N\exp\{-cA^3(\log\log N)^3\}.
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}
The $(\log\log N)^3$ exponent shape at polylogarithmic depth has the
near-simultaneous independent antecedent Dahan
\cite[Theorem~4.17]{Dahan} for a different two-parameter $(u,a)$
search-depth statistic without a genus gate; his printed $o(1)$ is
explicitly ineffective.  Equations (51.17)--(51.18) instead bound the
minimal congruence modulus $W$, and Lemmas~\ref{lem:effective-BV}--
\ref{lem:conductor-deletion} give effective constants subject to the
labels displayed here.  This priority distinction is part of the statement,
not an assertion that the statistics are interchangeable.

The formulas are interpreted beyond a fixed large threshold.  The same
bounds hold with cutoff $(\log p)^A$: split at $N^{1/2}$, use comparable
logarithms on the upper half, and absorb the lower half.  Define
\begin{equation}\tag{51.19}
 H_{\rm MOD}(A):\qquad W(p)\leq(\log p)^A
                 \quad\hbox{for every sufficiently large prime }p.
\end{equation}
Theorem~\ref{thm:W-lower} proves that this hypothesis is false for every
$0<A<1$; exactly the exponents $A\geq1$ remain open.  For any $A$ for which
it holds, this unproved pointwise hypothesis implies eventual prime
solvability by Lemma~\ref{lem:multiplier}; the upper tail does not make its
failure set empty.  It is not equivalent to $H_{\rm SPF}(A)$: the former is the Type-II
multiplier statistic (51.1), while the latter is the Type-I factor-in-one-
specified-grade assertion (50.7).  Neither hypothesis implies the other as
stated, although each implies an eventual representation.  Thus (39.35)
and $H_{\rm MOD}$ are respectively a tail and a pointwise question for the
same $W$; $H_{\rm SPF}$ is a different frontier.

\subsection{Effective class supply away from one conductor}
The lower half of the fixed-polylogarithmic supply uses
Bombieri--Vinogradov, not merely the elementary box calculation.  Large
composite progression moduli do not remove a possible Siegel zero, because
their characters may be induced from a small primitive conductor.  The
needed effective form is the following.

\begin{lemma}[effective Bombieri--Vinogradov away from one conductor;
standard cited input with dependency proof]\label{lem:effective-BV}
For every $A>0$ there are effectively computable $B,C,x_0$ such that, for
$x\geq x_0$ and $Q\leq x^{1/2}/(\log x)^B$, there is either no exceptional
conductor or one real primitive conductor $r_x\geq3$ for which
\begin{equation}\tag{51.20}
 \sum_{\substack{q\leq Q\\r_x\nmid q}}
 \max_{(a,q)=1}\left|\psi(x;q,a)-\frac{x}{\varphi(q)}\right|
 \leq\frac{Cx}{(\log x)^A}.
\end{equation}
The same statement holds for dyadic $\pi$-differences by effective partial
summation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Lenstra--Pomerance \cite[Lemma~11.2, pp.~1258--1260]
{LenstraPomerance} give computable absolute constants, one excluded
$s(x)\in[(\log x)^{1/2},e^{\sqrt{\log x}}]$, maxima over
$2\leq y\leq x$ and reduced residues, and error
$Cx^{1/2}Q(\log x)^5+Cxe^{-c\sqrt{\log x}}$ over $s(x)\nmid q$.
Restricting to $Q\leq x^{1/2}(\log x)^{-B}$, and enlarging smaller $Q$ to
$x^{1/3}\log x$, proves (51.20); the maximum in $y$ fixes one conductor
through a dyadic interval.

For completeness, set $R=(\log x)^{B_0}$.  Away from Page, the effective
explicit formula gives $|\psi(y,\chi^*)|\ll ye^{-c\sqrt{\log x}}$ uniformly
on $[x,2x]$, while
$\sum_{q\leq Q,\ r\mid q}1/\varphi(q)\ll\log(2Q)/\varphi(r)$.
At most $\varphi(r)$ primitive characters have conductor $r$, so all
$r\leq R$ cost $O(R\log(2Q))$; the exponential saving absorbs this.
Vaughan's identity and the large sieve handle larger conductors.  Fixed
conductors, including $4$, are checked effectively, and selecting Page at
$2x$ prevents two endpoint deletions.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[exceptional-conductor deletion tolerance; proved]
\label{lem:conductor-deletion}
In the boxes used for \eqref{eq:mult-mass}, let $r>1$ be a primitive real
character conductor.  Apart from the explicitly harmless conductor $4$,
there is a prime $p\mid r$ (or $p=2$ when the required extra factor is the
third power of $2$) such that
\begin{equation}\tag{51.21}
                  p\nmid uv\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r\nmid4uv.
\end{equation}
Uniformly in $c,k$, the pairs satisfying (51.21) retain a fixed positive
proportion of the harmonic box mass.  The low-$\omega$ cutoff may be chosen
so that it and (51.21) retain a fixed positive proportion simultaneously.
The same remains true after the low-congestion pruning of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A primitive real conductor is the absolute value of a fundamental
discriminant.  Choose an odd prime factor, or $p=2$ for conductor $8$;
conductor $4$ is handled effectively directly.  Fix $k$.  If $p\mid k$,
the condition $(uv,k)=1$ already excludes $p$.  If $p\nmid k$ and
$p\leq Ck$, the box count with the extra reduced conditions modulo $p$
retains the local proportion
\begin{equation}\tag{51.22}
 \frac{(1-1/p)^2}{1-1/p^2}=\frac{p-1}{p+1}\geq\frac13,
\end{equation}
and $H=K^{10}$ absorbs the boundary for combined modulus at most $Ck^2$.
If $p>Ck$, direct counting of the discarded pairs with $p\mid u$ or
$p\mid v$ costs $O(1/(pk))$ against main term
$\asymp\varphi(k)/k^2$, a ratio $O(k/(p\varphi(k)))=O(1/C)$; endpoint
errors are absorbed as before.  Choose $C$ large.  Increasing the fixed
Rankin cutoff makes the high-$\omega$ tail smaller than any prescribed
fixed fraction, and the high-incidence triples have $o(1)$ of the original
mass by Lemma~\ref{lem:incidence}.  Both deletions therefore leave the same
order of mass.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[effectivity of the repaired chains; proved internally,
record-adjacent and therefore \textsc{claimed/provisional}]
\label{thm:effectivity}
The constants in the source class-mass conclusions (16.8) and
(34.18)--(34.19), in source Theorems 16.4 and 39.7
(paper Theorems~\ref{thm:record} and~\ref{thm:three-quarter}), and in
Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-tail} may all be taken effectively computable.
The record statement retains every correctness and priority qualification
already attached to Theorem~\ref{thm:three-quarter}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
In each dyadic prime block apply Lemma~\ref{lem:effective-BV}; if its
